**Wire contract frozen by this checkpoint.** A Skeleton is a closed JSON object with `schema_version: 1`, `kind: "agent" | "workflow"`, non-empty string `id`, non-empty string `name`, `nodes` array and `edges` array; optional `description` is a string. A node is a closed object with non-empty string `id`, one closed `type`, and required `config` JSON object. `config` is deliberately opaque in CP-3: later node contracts will define its per-type fields without weakening this envelope. An edge is a closed object with non-empty string `id`, `kind: "flow" | "attach"`, non-empty `source_node_id`, `source_port_id`, `target_node_id`, and `target_port_id` strings. Node and edge IDs are unique; every edge endpoint names an admitted node. The validator returns a detached JSON copy.

The admitted node type set is `start`, `end`, `agent`, `toolkit_pool`, `subagent_pool`, `if_else`, `switch_case`, `tool_call`, `skill_read`, `code`, `workflow_call`, and `hooks_pool`. `agent` is the persisted name for the Kernel Loop node, retaining the established graph type and avoiding ambiguity with root `kind: agent`. A Workflow may admit only `start`, `end`, `if_else`, `switch_case`, `tool_call`, `skill_read`, `code`, and `workflow_call`; it rejects `agent`, `toolkit_pool`, `subagent_pool`, and `hooks_pool` at `nodes[i].type`. This enforces the platform graph boundary only. As agreed, it does not inspect arbitrary trusted Python or third-party tools to prove they never make an external model request.

**Draft boundary.** Empty node/edge arrays, missing Start/End, disconnected nodes, port compatibility, DAG rules, one-Start/one-End, Agent's required Kernel Loop, bindings, config semantics, resource compatibility and run/publish eligibility are intentionally deferred to CP-4. An edge that names a nonexistent node is malformed syntax, not an incomplete draft, and is rejected here. The old Recipe parser and graph compiler stay untouched.

### CP-4 implementation handoff (2026-09-26)

**Goal.** Make the D-07 distinction executable without changing storage or starting a runner: a well-formed Skeleton remains saveable even while it has incomplete graph structure, while structural preflight reports every known blocking defect and refuses it. This checkpoint freezes the graph portion of Run/Publish eligibility. It does not claim that a structurally complete document has validated per-node configuration, resolved a model/resource/binding, or can yet be executed.

**Public API.** Create `SkeletonDiagnostic(code, path, message)` as an immutable diagnostic record. `diagnose_skeleton_draft(document) -> tuple[SkeletonDiagnostic, ...]` first applies CP-3 schema validation; malformed documents still raise `SkeletonValidationError` and are not saveable. A schema-valid draft returns all deterministic structural diagnostics in document order. `validate_skeleton_for_execution(document) -> dict` runs the same diagnosis and raises the first diagnostic as `SkeletonValidationError` if any exist; otherwise it returns the detached CP-3 document. The diagnostic code and path therefore identify the same problem for the UI and strict preflight, without turning diagnostics into exceptions during normal draft saving.

**Structural completion contract.** Both graph kinds require exactly one Start and End. Agent requires at least one persisted `agent` (Kernel Loop); Workflow continues to have none through CP-3's kind rule. Pools (`toolkit_pool`, `subagent_pool`, `hooks_pool`) never participate in flow and each must attach to an Agent. An attach edge uses `attach_top`/`attach_bot` ports and connects exactly an Agent and a pool. A flow edge connects executable nodes, targets `in`, and may not originate at End or target Start. Normal execution nodes use `out`; If/Else uses its required `true` and `false` outlets; Switch has a required `default` outlet and may use its stable case IDs as additional outlets. An outlet may have one flow edge; several flow edges may target one `in` for exclusive rejoin.

The flow graph is an exclusive DAG. Start has no flow input and End has no flow output. Every executable non-Start node needs a flow input, every executable non-End node needs a flow output, each executable node must be reachable from Start and must be able to reach End, and cycles are diagnostics. The validator does not infer branch selection, wait for rejoin, parse a condition AST, validate Switch case IDs/values, or inspect opaque node `config`; those contracts are added in their respective node/runtime checkpoints. A syntactically valid but incomplete graph is therefore saveable, but `validate_skeleton_for_execution` rejects it.

**Known boundary.** “Run/Publish” in this CP means the frozen graph-structure gate, not a promise that runtime execution is already available. Missing Agent model selection, bindings, result schema, tool/resource reference, Workflow revision and node-specific configuration cannot be diagnosed until their wire contracts exist. They remain blocking requirements for the eventual Run/Publish path and must be added before runtime wiring. The validator also does not attempt to prove that trusted Code or third-party tools never make an external model call.

**Allowed edits.** Create only `src/unchain/skeleton/diagnostics.py`, `src/unchain/skeleton/validation.py`, `tests/test_skeleton_validation.py`, and declarative fixtures under `tests/fixtures/skeleton_validation/`; update the new untracked `src/unchain/skeleton/__init__.py` to export the three public CP-4 symbols. Do not modify CP-1–CP-3 modules or tests, legacy Recipe/graph code, providers, stores, UI, dependencies, manifests, loaders or persistence.

**Impact and safety.** The three CP-4 symbols are absent from the base GitNexus index, so their impact is UNKNOWN; source search confirms they are new and have no runtime callers. `validate_skeleton_document` is likewise unindexed because the package is still isolated; CP-4 consumes it but does not alter it. Existing PuPu Recipe compiler rules are a legacy straight-chain implementation and are deliberately not reused or edited. This checkpoint does not cross a persistence/provider/process boundary, so BC-001/BC-002 remain definition evidence only; all runtime BC/SEQ/AC are PENDING/NOT_RUN.

**Tests and stop condition.** Consume fixtures for a complete Agent graph with a pool, a complete non-LLM Workflow, and an incomplete draft. Cover empty/missing/multiple Start/End, missing Agent Kernel Loop, each invalid flow/attach endpoint and port, missing If true/false and Switch default outlets, duplicate outlet, pool without Agent attachment, flow input/output absence, unreachable/cannot-reach-End nodes, cycle, exclusive branch rejoin, diagnostic order and exact paths, strict preflight's first error, detached result, fresh-process provider-free import and legacy runtime/store absence. ### CP-5 implementation handoff (2026-09-26)

**Goal.** Freeze a provider-free, in-memory conversion bridge for the three historical formats. The bridge produces a new v1 `kind: agent` Skeleton only. It does not inspect directories, write files, alter a loader, select a default resource, resolve references, expand built-in prompts, create a migration marker, or activate any converted definition. This makes conversion reviewable before the host owns the durable cutover.

**Public API.** Create `convert_legacy_definition(source_kind, source, *, resource_id) -> dict` in `unchain.skeleton.legacy`, and export it from `unchain.skeleton`.

- `source_kind` is exactly `recipe`, `skeleton_template`, or `soul`; the caller must choose it explicitly. A new v1 Skeleton is never accepted through this legacy entry point.
- `resource_id` is a nonempty string supplied by the host and becomes the new root `id` unchanged. Conversion never derives it from a display name, path, or random value.
- `source` is an in-memory JSON object for `recipe` and `skeleton_template`, and a UTF-8-decoded `.soul` text string for `soul`. Inputs must be strict JSON where applicable. The converter neither reads nor modifies the old source.
- Every success returns a detached document accepted by `validate_skeleton_document`; it is deliberately not a promise that the definition is runnable. Invalid or lossy source raises `SkeletonValidationError` with a stable code/path and no submitted value in the message.

**Envelope and preservation protocol.** All three conversions produce one Start, one or more Kernel Loops (`agent`), and one End, rooted at `schema_version: 1`, `kind: agent`. The Start config carries a detached, lossless provenance record under `legacy_source`: recipe/template JSON is retained as `document`, and Soul retains its original `text`. This record prevents silent loss of fields that have no v1 node-config contract yet; it is metadata for a later host bridge and never an instruction to execute legacy syntax.

A graphless Recipe, old template Skeleton, or Soul becomes fixed IDs `start`, `agent`, `end` with `start → agent → end`. A Recipe that already has nonempty `nodes`/`edges` preserves node IDs and edge IDs. `toolpool` normalizes to `toolkit_pool`; legacy omitted/unknown edge `kind` is derived exactly as the historical parser did; an old reversed flow `in → out` is normalized to canonical `out → in`. Each retained node places its original non-identity fields under `config.legacy_fields`; compatibility root Recipe fields are placed on the first Kernel Loop under `config.legacy_recipe`. Existing graph IDs, edge IDs, explicit bindings, capability references and unknown historical fields therefore remain recoverable without widening the v1 schema.

**Prompt protocol.** Each Kernel Loop has a direct `config.prompt`: `{kind: text, text: ...}` for an old Soul prompt, or for a valid old JSON Skeleton prompt after extracting its string `instructions`; invalid JSON Skeleton prompts and non-string `instructions` fail explicitly. The historical Default sentinel is detected with its optional leading Start exports and becomes `{kind: builtin_developer_prompt}`. Its original text remains in `legacy_source`; a later host migration must expand and persist the frozen prompt snapshot before runnable cutover. No `prompt_format` field is emitted in v1.

**Template protocol.** Old `.skeleton` and `.soul` template metadata is normalized under the Kernel Loop's `legacy_template`: allowed modes, output mode, memory policy, parallel safety, allowed tools and model. Soul uses the historical explicit defaults: `[delegate, worker]`, `summary`, `ephemeral`, `true`, and no model/tools unless frontmatter supplies tools/model. Its frontmatter grammar is intentionally the old minimal grammar only: bare scalar values and `[a, b]` lists, required `name`/`description`, and a nonempty body. Unknown frontmatter and template fields are still retained in the Start provenance record. A converted template is an Agent definition; it is not inserted into a Subagent Pool in this checkpoint.

**Recipe protocol.** A Recipe follows the historical parser's persistence defaults: `description`, `model`, `max_iterations`, `toolkits`, `subagent_pool`, `merge_with_user_selected`, and `subagent_profile` may be absent where the old parser supplies its documented default. A graphless Recipe still requires its root Agent. A nonempty old graph may omit the root Agent because the historical parser projects its first graph Agent. Each graph Kernel Loop derives its prompt only from that node's `override`: an omitted override produces the historical empty Soul prompt, and an override without `prompt_format` defaults to Soul. It never inherits the root Agent prompt. A supplied root Agent must still be valid even when a graph exists. The full root compatibility record remains on the first Kernel Loop. Any `schema_version` makes a JSON object ineligible for legacy conversion, so a future Skeleton cannot be downgraded or reinterpreted. This checkpoint does not interpret or resolve toolkit IDs, template names, `recipe_ref`, inline subagents, bindings or model configuration; it preserves them exactly for the later resource/runtime bridge.

**Allowed edits.** Create only `src/unchain/skeleton/legacy.py`, `tests/test_skeleton_legacy.py`, and declarative JSON fixtures under `tests/fixtures/skeleton_legacy/`; update the new untracked `src/unchain/skeleton/__init__.py` to export the converter; update this handoff only with the actual CP-5 result. Do not change CP-1–CP-4 modules/tests, any legacy recipe/template loader, graph compiler, provider, runtime, store, UI, package manifest or dependency. No active migration or external writes.

**Impact and safety.** `convert_legacy_definition` is a new unindexed symbol, so GitNexus reports UNKNOWN; source search confirms it has no existing caller. The existing `recipe.py`, `subagent_loader.py`, `recipe_loader.py`, and `unchain_adapter.py` are read-only behavior references and must not be imported by the new package. CP-5 remains provider-free and crosses no persistence/process boundary; the eventual host migration still owns BC-009 and related end-to-end evidence.

**Tests and stop condition.** Fixtures must drive conversion of: a graphless Recipe with toolkits/subagents/profile and the builtin sentinel; a graph Recipe retaining IDs, bindings, capability references, `toolpool`, inferred edge kind and reversed flow normalization; a complete old JSON template; and a Soul with explicit tools/model and a Soul with defaults. Assert provider-free import and no legacy loader/runtime/store import. ### CP-6 implementation handoff — provider-free Workflow preparation (2026-09-26)

**Goal.** Establish the real preparation branch that a later `WorkflowRunner` will consume, while keeping this checkpoint smaller than a runner. A structurally runnable `kind: workflow` document must become an immutable provider-free graph plan without constructing or importing `Agent`, `AgentBuilder`, `KernelLoop`, `ModelIOFactoryRegistry`, provider adapters, or provider SDKs, and without reading an API-key environment variable. This is the bounded feasibility evidence required before graph execution work begins. It does not execute a node, resolve a tool/resource, persist a checkpoint, initialize a model, alter the current Agent path, or claim AC-012 is complete.

**Public API.** Add `PreparedWorkflowPlan`, `PreparedFlowEdge`, and `prepare_workflow(document) -> PreparedWorkflowPlan` in `unchain.skeleton.preparation`, exported from `unchain.skeleton`.

- `prepare_workflow` first applies the existing strict Run/Publish structural preflight. It accepts only `kind: workflow`; a valid Agent document fails with structured code `wrong_kind` at `("document", "kind")`. Schema-invalid and structurally incomplete inputs retain the existing validator's exact error code/path.
- The plan carries only definition metadata, stable node IDs in document order, a deterministic topological order, immutable flow-edge records in document order, and an immutable encoded definition snapshot. `snapshot()` returns a fresh detached JSON object on every call. Mutating the source or a returned snapshot cannot change the plan or later snapshots.
- Topological order is a compile-time ordering aid, not a promise to execute both branch arms. Ties follow original node order. CP-4 already rejects cycles, missing flow endpoints and pool attachments before the plan is built. No handler/resource/model capability resolution is added here.
- The API has no provider/model/API-key/factory argument and no fallback to Agent preparation. A Workflow document containing strings named `model`, `provider`, or `api_key` inside an otherwise admitted Code config still cannot cause provider discovery or credential reads during preparation; platform LLM capability admission remains enforced by the Workflow node-type boundary.

Do not edit CP-1–CP-5 modules/tests/fixtures, `unchain.agent`, `unchain.kernel`, provider code, runtime manifest, stores, loaders, PuPu product code, package metadata, or dependencies.

**Impact and safety.** GitNexus reports `AgentBuilder` LOW impact (4 direct importers, 28 upstream symbols) and `ModelIOFactoryRegistry` MEDIUM impact (4 direct importers, 30 upstream symbols); both remain read-only. No commit, push, PR, runner, execution, persistence, model initialization, sidecar wiring, audit or rollout. Stop for independent GPT-6 review and owner acceptance before CP-7.

### CP-7 implementation handoff — versioned Workflow recovery envelope (2026-09-26)

**Goal.** Freeze the provider-free, in-memory recovery record that a later WorkflowRunner will persist and replay. It records exactly one active exclusive-DAG Workflow invocation: the immutable definition/topology identity, completed node receipts, chosen flow edges, a pending interaction or explicitly unknown external outcome, one invocation stack, and an End-only terminal receipt. It does not write a journal, resume a process, execute a node, retry a tool/model, resolve a resource, invoke a provider, alter the old checkpoint reader, or claim cold recovery is operational.

**Why this is a new contract.** Existing `context.graph_checkpoint.GraphExecutionPlan` and `GraphStepBinding` are read-only references. GitNexus gives both LOW upstream impact (three direct importing modules, 13 total), but their validated shape requires provider/model on every step, contiguous indexes and an immediate-predecessor chain. That is incompatible with a pure Workflow and an exclusive branch/rejoin graph. CP-7 must not add an opt-out flag or weaken that old reader. The new envelope is isolated under `unchain.skeleton`; the later persistence adapter owns bridge/writer work.

**Public API.** Create `RecoveryEnvelopeError`, `build_workflow_recovery_envelope`, `validate_workflow_recovery_envelope`, and `workflow_definition_digest` in `unchain.skeleton.recovery`, exported by `unchain.skeleton`.

- The root is a CLOSED JSON object with exactly `schema`, `definition`, `invocation`, `call_stack`, `completed_nodes`, `selected_edges`, `active`, and `terminal`. The sole accepted schema is `unchain.skeleton_workflow_recovery.v1`; unknown/malformed versions fail closed.
- `definition` is closed `{id, definition_digest, topology_digest}`. Both digests are SHA-256 hex. They are calculated from the checked Workflow plan: definition digest is canonical JSON of the complete validated snapshot; topology digest is canonical JSON of its document-order node IDs and flow edges. Validation always receives the actual `PreparedWorkflowPlan` and rejects mismatched IDs/digests before accepting any receipt.
- `invocation` is closed `{execution_id, graph_invocation_id, attempt_id}`. All are nonempty identifiers. `call_stack` is a nonempty closed frame array. Its final frame must equal the current invocation and definition; preceding frames preserve the parent graph invocation, definition identity/digest and required caller node invocation ID. This checkpoint validates shape and lineage uniqueness, but does not resolve parent resources or execute nested calls.
- Each `completed_nodes` item is closed `{node_id, node_invocation_id, attempt_id, input_receipt_id, output_receipt_id}`. It may contain no provider/model fields. Node IDs are unique, appear in the checked plan, and form one path beginning at Start. Every completed non-End node has exactly one matching selected edge; no selected edge may originate from an incomplete node, duplicate a source, or differ from the checked plan edge endpoints. This persists the chosen path, so replay never re-evaluates an If/Switch.
- All node invocation IDs and attempt IDs are unique across completed and active records, and no node attempt may equal a graph-frame attempt. Input/output receipt IDs identify newly persisted node receipts and are unique across completed records; the End terminal is the one intentional reference to its End output receipt, not a second receipt. Receipt payloads/artifacts may be shared later through explicit artifact references, but receipt identity is never reused as an alias. All call-stack frames share the root execution ID; graph invocation IDs, frame attempt IDs and parent caller-node invocation IDs are unique. These constraints prevent a recovery reader from joining two different graph actions by a reused identifier.
- `active` is `null` or a closed nonterminal node record. `waiting_interaction` carries `interaction_id` and `request_receipt_id`; `outcome_unknown` carries `intent_receipt_id`. Its node is the next node selected by the completed path and cannot already be completed. An unknown outcome is a terminal stop for automatic replay: the validator never converts it to pending/runnable.
- `terminal` is `null` or the closed `{kind: "end_completed", node_id, node_invocation_id, end_receipt_id}` record. It is accepted only when no active node exists, its node is the graph End, the completed path reaches that End, and its receipt exactly equals End's output receipt. No final result may be inferred from the last non-End node.
- Successful calls return a detached canonical JSON object. Inputs are strict bounded JSON under the CP-1 rules; mutations of source, plan snapshots, or returned envelope cannot alter another returned envelope. This protocol is Workflow-only in CP-7, so it deliberately contains no provider/model variant or fake provider field; Agent/Kernal Loop recovery is a later extension.

**Allowed edits.** Create only `src/unchain/skeleton/recovery.py`, `tests/test_skeleton_recovery.py`, and declarative JSON fixtures under `tests/fixtures/skeleton_recovery/`; update the untracked `src/unchain/skeleton/__init__.py` only for CP-7 exports; append only the actual result to this handoff. Do not edit CP-1–CP-6 files, `context/graph_checkpoint.py`, journals, persistence, Agent/Kernel/provider code, manifests, PuPu, dependencies, or packaging.

**Boundary record.** This supplies the static, in-memory part of BC-011 / SEQ-009 and AC-012. Producer/consumer today is `PreparedWorkflowPlan + future durable graph writer` → this strict envelope validator + future recovery dispatcher. The boundary is VERSIONED and CLOSED. No exact deployed artifact pair or durable-state sequence has run, so all journal/store/cold-restart/old-binary evidence stays NOT_RUN. The old reader remains unchanged and must fail closed on a new envelope; the new validator must reject an old `unchain.graph_execution_plan.v1` descriptor rather than guessing a conversion.

**Tests and stop condition.** Use a complete Workflow with an If branch and rejoin to exercise a partial chosen path, a waiting interaction, an `outcome_unknown` node, and End completion. Prove canonical detachment and digest sensitivity to config/topology changes; selection/receipt ordering; Start/path/End constraints; branch edge mismatch; duplicate/foreign node or invocation identity; unknown fields/versions; provider/model field rejection; malformed/null/non-JSON values; invalid call-stack lineage; waiting/unknown/terminal conflicts; old/new mutual reader rejection; and a fresh-process provider-free import guard. Run the full CP-1–CP-7 focused suite. No commit, push, PR, journal/store writer, sidecar wiring, runner, model, active recovery, audit or rollout. Stop for independent GPT-6 review and owner acceptance before CP-8.

### CP-7 owner-requested acceptance review — REQUEST CORRECTION (2026-09-26)

This supersedes the ready-for-acceptance recommendation above. The strong parent independently reran the full suite (`123 passed in 0.89s`), exercised 18 valid waiting/unknown/End states across both branches with JSON round trips and output isolation, and checked all 1,956 nonempty nonrepeating node sequences against an independent terminal-path oracle. Exactly the two complete Start-to-End branch paths were accepted. Deep forged snapshot failures are correctly normalized by digest, builder and validator. No path/End or recursion regression was found.

**P2 — Recovery digest narrows the accepted CP-1 JSON domain and depends on host interpreter settings.** `src/unchain/skeleton/recovery.py:49–53` uses Python's ordinary decimal integer JSON encoder. CP-1 explicitly retains `10 ** 5000` (`tests/test_skeleton_io.py:162`), and placing that value in a Code config passes `prepare_workflow`. Yet `workflow_definition_digest` rejects this valid plan as `invalid_json` at `("plan", "snapshot")` under the default 4,300-digit conversion limit. With that limit disabled in the isolated probe process, the same plan successfully produces a digest. Thus an admitted Workflow cannot reliably enter the recovery protocol across Python configurations. The structured exception prevents a crash but does not fix the domain mismatch.

Required correction: canonical integer encoding must preserve CP-1's accepted values independently of the process-wide integer conversion setting, without changing the host's global setting. Retain regression coverage for digest, envelope build and validation at the default and a lower supported limit, including identical digest results. Do not silently narrow CP-1 or CP-6 during this fix. No production source changed in this review; CP-8 remains unstarted and all durable/runtime evidence remains NOT_RUN.

### CP-7 correction result — ready for owner re-acceptance (2026-09-26)

The recovery digest now uses a private deterministic JSON byte encoder rather than Python's process-limited integer-to-decimal path. Arbitrary admitted integers are emitted in decimal chunks without reading or changing `sys.int_max_str_digits`; strings retain ordinary Unicode scalar values as UTF-8 while escaping each explicit surrogate codepoint independently. This preserves the CP-1 value domain and prevents a single non-BMP scalar from sharing an identity with two explicit surrogate codepoints. The correction changes only `src/unchain/skeleton/recovery.py` and `tests/test_skeleton_recovery.py`.

Retained tests cover positive and negative 5,000-digit integers, a lone surrogate, identical definition identities under the original, 640-digit and unlimited interpreter settings, public envelope build/validation, and distinct digests for scalar-versus-surrogate values and object keys. The parent reran CP-1–CP-7 with `125 passed in 0.66s`, checked 440 arbitrary-integer encodings against Python with its limit disabled, 13,496 distinct mixed-string encodings for collisions, 10,000 non-surrogate strings against the standard JSON encoder, and a 99,999-item canonical array. No production code changes the interpreter's global digit setting.

Independent GPT-6 review exercised 2,433 standard-encoder differential cases, 66,536 character/escape probes, public digest/build/validation and exact node/depth boundaries. It reran the full suite with `125 passed in 0.68s` and returned **ACCEPT FOR OWNER CHECKPOINT REVIEW**. Its resource probes measured a 100,000-digit integer at 0.227 seconds and approximately 1.55 MB peak allocation, and a 99,900-element array at approximately 17.8 MB. Integer conversion remains superlinear for exceptionally large integers, but no practical or contractual blocker was found. This isolated package is not wired into the sidecar, so no running sidecar was restarted. No commit, push, PR, CP-8 work, runtime integration, audit or rollout occurred.

### CP-8 implementation handoff — pure Workflow graph runner (2026-09-26)

**Goal.** Implement the first executable M1 slice: a provider-free, in-memory `WorkflowRunner` for a structurally valid `kind: workflow` plan. It executes one exclusive path from Start to End, evaluates If/Else and Switch using the accepted CP-1/CP-2 contracts, invokes only host-injected deterministic handlers, and returns only End's declared exports. This is deliberately not durable execution: no journal, recovery-envelope writer/reader, identity lease, cancellation, artifact, sidecar, model, provider, tool executor, skill reader, Code subprocess, Workflow call, Agent graph, memory or root-chat completion is wired in CP-8.

**Why the existing graph runner is out of scope.** GitNexus confirms `GraphExecutionPlan` and `GraphCheckpointService` have three direct importing modules (`graph_harness.py`, `request_factory.py`, `sqlite_generation_rebase_v2.py`) and implement the old linear provider/model-backed step protocol. Their contiguous predecessors and provider/model requirements conflict with a pure Workflow branch/rejoin. They remain read-only. The new runner consumes `PreparedWorkflowPlan` under `unchain.skeleton`; it does not import the legacy graph checkpoint or any provider/Agent/Kernel module.

**Public API.** Create `src/unchain/skeleton/execution.py`, exported by `unchain.skeleton`:

- `WorkflowExecutionError(SkeletonValidationError)` for machine-readable, path-bearing runner failures.
- `WorkflowNodeContext(document_id, node_id, node_type)` and `WorkflowNodeCompleted(result, selected_port="out")`. A host handler receives `(context, resolved_inputs)` and must return this completion with a strict JSON-object result. A handler error, a noncompletion, non-JSON result, or non-`out` port is a failed run; no partial End result is returned.
- `WorkflowRunResult(outputs, completed_node_ids, selected_edge_ids)`, all occurrence-detached. Only a successfully completed End produces `outputs`.
- `run_workflow(plan, inputs, handlers) -> WorkflowRunResult`. `plan` is re-prepared from its snapshot and compared to prevent manually assembled plans from bypassing preflight. `inputs` is a strict JSON object. `handlers` maps permitted non-control Workflow node types (`code`, `tool_call`, `skill_read`, `workflow_call`) to explicit host callables; CP-8 ships none. A missing handler fails closed rather than falling back to a model, tool or Code runtime.

**Execution-config wire for this slice.** CP-3 intentionally leaves outer `node.config` opaque. CP-8 therefore reads only a CLOSED `config.execution` child and leaves all sibling node-specific fields for their owning later checkpoint. Every node must have this child; it is preflighted for every node before the first handler runs, including an unselected branch.

- Start: `{outputs}`. It maps the root `inputs` as its result through CP-1 output mappings and selects `out`.
- End: `{inputs, outputs}`. It resolves its bindings from completed selected-path exports, maps only from its resolved inputs, and terminates. It never calls a handler and cannot select an edge.
- If/Else: `{inputs, condition}`. It resolves inputs, evaluates the existing closed condition AST and selects exactly `true` or `false`.
- Switch: `{inputs, value, cases}`. `value` is an existing closed input/literal operand; existing strict `select_case` chooses a case ID or `default`.
- Host-handler nodes: `{inputs, outputs}`. Their handler result maps through existing CP-1 output mappings and may select only `out`.

The runner preflights bindings/mappings/value expressions, exact control config fields, duplicate names and every control outlet. If/Switch must have exactly the applicable flow outlet(s); Switch flow outlets must be `default` or one declared case ID. It tracks completed exports by node ID only on the selected path, so skipped branches are unavailable to a normal reference and usable solely through the existing `first_present` rule. A rejoin executes once because the runner follows one selected edge at a time. CP-8 validates strict JSON and declared mappings, not final user JSON Schema dialects or resource-specific result schemas; those remain M3/M4/M6 work.

**Boundary record.** This implements the static execution portion of BC-011 and AC-012 only: `PreparedWorkflowPlan + root inputs + host handler completion` → closed in-memory `WorkflowRunResult`. Admission is CLOSED for `config.execution` variants and VERSIONED only through the already checked plan definition. Unknown/malformed config, foreign/mutated plans, missing handler, bad handler result, selected absent outlet and a foreign edge fail before an End result. No receipt is durable and no resume/cold-restart claim is made, so SEQ-009's persistence states remain NOT_RUN.

**Allowed edits.** Create only `src/unchain/skeleton/execution.py`, `tests/test_skeleton_execution.py`, declarative fixtures in `tests/fixtures/skeleton_execution/`, and exports in the existing untracked `src/unchain/skeleton/__init__.py`; append only the actual result to this handoff. Do not edit CP-1–CP-7 modules/tests, legacy graph checkpoint/journal/persistence, Agent/Kernel/provider/tool/Code/sidecar/PuPu code, manifests, packaging or dependencies. New symbols are absent from the base GitNexus index (UNKNOWN); source search must confirm no active runtime callers before implementation. Existing old graph symbols are read-only LOW-impact references. No commit, push, PR, sidecar restart, runtime wiring, audit or rollout.

**Tests and stop condition.** Fixtures drive: Start → If → left/right Code → rejoin → End; Start → Switch → cases/default → End; direct null versus missing/`first_present`; and a handler node whose result is mapped into End. Test both branch choices, exactly-one rejoin, stable completed/selected trace, output/source/returned-result detachment, strict root input, config/config-branch validation before handler execution, missing handler, handler exception/noncompletion/non-JSON/custom port, malformed/foreign plan, unavailable selected outlet, Switch unknown/undeclared outlet, End-only output, and a fresh-process import guard that blocks Agent, Kernel, provider/SDK, Tool executor, journal/persistence and environment credential reads. Run the full CP-1–CP-8 focused suite. Terra stops after this bounded implementation; GPT-6 independently reviews; the owner accepts or corrects before CP-9. CP-9 may only address durable journal/recovery integration, never silently extend this runner.

Only End exports become the returned `WorkflowRunResult`; returned outputs and trace values are detached. The runner contains no default executor and does not import or initialize Agent, Kernel, provider, SDK, Tool, journal, persistence, credential or sidecar machinery.

Independent review initially found four production defects: skipped branches were not fully preflighted, Switch declarations and outlets could diverge, hostile configuration types leaked native exceptions, and End accepted a fabricated `result` source. The implementation was corrected within CP-8. Review then required the planned durable test evidence rather than one-off probes: declarative Switch/default and explicit Code-rejoin fixtures, null/missing bindings, foreign-plan and skipped-branch guards, handler failure modes, and an active fresh-process isolation check. These are now retained tests. The final independent review verified 56 caught string/byte credential-access probes across eight environment access channels, ten caught blocked imports, and demonstrated the isolation test actually executes `run_workflow`; it also confirmed that removing checked-plan or JSON-path validation makes retained tests fail.

The parent independently ran the focused CP-1–CP-8 suite with `PYTHONPATH=src pytest -q` and obtained **136 passed in 0.93s**. The final independent review obtained **136 passed in 0.86s**, verified the declarative `Start → If → left/right Code → shared Code rejoin → End` fixture is consumed without test-time graph rewriting, and found no tracked or staged scope drift. This accepts only BC-011 / AC-012's static in-memory execution portion. Journal writing, durable recovery, identity leases, restart, retries, cancellation, sidecar/runtime wiring and deployed-artifact evidence remain NOT_RUN; CP-9 must address those separately. No commit, push, PR, audit, rollout or CP-9 work occurred.

### CP-8 owner-requested re-acceptance — REQUEST CORRECTION (2026-09-26)

Eight dataflow/repeated-run isolation checks passed. A further 1,107 JSON configuration mutations produced no native exception leaks (46 remained valid variants). No additional execution or isolation defect was reproduced.

**P2 — Runner diagnostics lose node identity and mix incompatible path formats.** At `execution.py:177–200`, errors from `resolve_inputs`, `map_outputs`, condition evaluation and Switch evaluation are forwarded with helper-local paths. Static Switch errors have the same problem: an array-valued case reports `("cases", 0, "value")` from line 138, while a missing case outlet reports `("document", "nodes", "switch", "config", "execution", "cases")` from line 171, using a string ID where the document contains an array. The public path-bearing error contract therefore cannot reliably identify the graph node/field for a caller or Builder diagnostic.

Required correction within CP-8: normalize helper failures at the node boundary, retaining the original code while attaching unambiguous node identity and a consistent document-field path (use numeric node positions for document arrays, or explicitly separate node identity from field paths). Include Switch case preflight/outlet failures, binding/output/condition failures, and handler/result errors in the same convention. Retain tests proving identical failures in different nodes are distinguishable and config paths resolve to the intended field. ### CP-9 implementation handoff — durable recovery descriptors (2026-09-26)

Ticket #356, existing isolated clones/branches and bases above remain authoritative. This is the first bounded M2 slice, not completion of M2. Goal: persist the already validated CP-7 paused/unknown/End recovery descriptor in the existing execution journal, reload it in a fresh process against the actual prepared definition, and reject stale concurrent updates. CP-8 runner remains untouched. Receipt IDs remain references: this checkpoint neither writes nor verifies receipt payloads, snapshots the definition, nor authorizes resume. No runnable/ready state is invented to fit CP-7. No provider, sidecar, tool, retry, cancellation, root-memory or UI integration.

**Research and reuse.** GitNexus context confirms `BoundExecutionJournal` is the existing journal boundary; `SQLiteContextV2Store.bind_execution` supplies its real SQLite implementation. `append_with_artifacts(request=..., artifacts=(), precondition=...)` already does exact-operation replay, then snapshot/precondition/event append in a single immediate transaction. Reuse this capability with no non-atomic fallback and no SQLite/journal schema changes. New Skeleton symbols remain unindexed (UNKNOWN); source search confirms only isolated exports/tests, no deployed consumers. Perform impact before edits; do not change old checkpoint readers or the shared journal implementation.

**Chosen interface.** Add `unchain.skeleton.recovery_journal` (explicit submodule import; do not eagerly import journal from Skeleton `__init__`, preserving CP-1–8 import isolation). Public `save_workflow_recovery(journal, plan, envelope, *, attempt, operation_id, expected_revision)` returns a journal append result; `load_workflow_recovery(journal, plan, *, graph_invocation_id)` returns `None` or a detached record containing `revision`, `envelope`, and persisted event identity. `attempt` is the existing `AttemptRef`: execution and attempt IDs must exactly equal envelope invocation and bound journal; never silently normalize envelope identities. Journal-native IDs retain the existing identifier restrictions; report unsupported native IDs explicitly. Graph invocation and all embedded CP-7 strings retain exact codepoints. The supplied generation is frozen across revisions of that graph invocation. Operation/event IDs must be deterministic for an exact retry, scoped without collisions across graph invocations; an operation reused with different data must fail through the existing journal conflict contract.

**Wire contract (BC-011 scoped extension).** Event type `skeleton.workflow_recovery.saved`, CLOSED payload schema `unchain.skeleton_workflow_recovery_journal.v1`: schema, graph key (SHA-256 of exact graph ID codepoints), revision, previous-event identity (null at revision 1), codec, encoded envelope, envelope SHA-256. Use explicit `json-utf8-surrogatepass-base64.v1`: canonical sorted compact JSON with ensure_ascii=False, allow_nan=False, UTF-8 surrogatepass, then strict base64. CP-7 envelopes have no arbitrary numeric payloads; this preserves scalar Unicode, paired explicit surrogates and lone surrogates distinctly, unlike ensure_ascii=True JSON. Decoder requires canonical re-encoding equality, known codec/schema/exact fields, bounded encoded and decoded bytes (8 MiB decoded maximum), duplicate-key rejection and valid CP-7 shape against the caller's actual plan. Encoded transport is not encryption; descriptor metadata only, never arbitrary data/credentials. Fail closed on malformed matching recovery events; validate all recovery-event headers before filtering, so malformed identities cannot hide an event. Never fall back to an older valid checkpoint after corruption. Bounded journal snapshots must fail on overflow, never return a partial latest state.

**Revision/transition contract.** expected_revision is an exact nonnegative integer (not bool). Initial write requires absence/revision 0; subsequent write is exactly previous+1 under transactional precondition. Reader validates the whole matching chain, not only its tail, including event scope, operation content integrity, revision/previous-event links, immutable definition/invocation/call_stack/generation and CP-7 validity. Completed records and selected edges are append-only exact prefixes. The prior active node's node_id/node_invocation_id/attempt_id must remain identical if still active, or become the first newly completed node with those identities. A still-waiting active record cannot change its interaction/request identity; it may advance to outcome_unknown. Once outcome_unknown or End-terminal is recorded, new revisions are refused (resolution protocol is future work); exact operation retry still returns the original event. No-op new revisions are refused. All preconditions apply to real transactional writes, not an earlier read. Snapshot failures, unsupported atomic append and repository errors propagate explicitly; validation has stable code/path errors.

**SEQ-013 (CP-9 only).** checked plan + envelope -> encode/validate -> journal immediate transaction -> exact retry check -> complete snapshot + chain/expected-revision/transition check -> single event commit -> fresh-process read -> strict chain/decode/plan validation. Loser in a same-revision race writes no event. This does not claim SEQ-009 child/Hook resume or AC-012 full durable execution. Disk atomicity is inherited from existing journal; prove adapter rejection/rollback and retry behavior using the actual store. Exact deployed wheel/sidecar pair remains NOT_RUN.

The transactional precondition must also validate the projected post-append snapshot against existing event/byte limits, rejecting before commit rather than creating a record that its own bounded reader cannot load. Existing SQLite imports transitively load provider modules/SDKs; that legacy import coupling is unchanged here. The new adapter itself must import without them. Real SQLite save/load must not initialize a model client or read credentials; removing the legacy store import coupling belongs to later integration, and CP-9 must not claim an entirely SDK-free concrete-store import.

**Allowed files and suitability.** Terra implementation is suitable after the above decisions: create only `src/unchain/skeleton/recovery_journal.py`, `tests/test_skeleton_recovery_journal.py`, optional declarative fixtures under `tests/fixtures/skeleton_recovery_journal/`; append results here. No edits to CP-1–8, existing journal/store, manifests, dependencies, PuPu code or main trees. Strong parent owns deviations and review. Read repo instructions and required impact first. If the real journal cannot implement the frozen contract, report before dependent changes.

**Retained acceptance tests.** Use real SQLite producer/consumer, never an in-memory fake as durability proof. Cover no record, waiting/unknown/terminal round trips, close/reopen and actual child-process read, detached returns, two independent store connections/processes racing revision 0/1 (exactly one winner), lost-response exact replay including after a later revision, different-body operation conflict, foreign execution/attempt/generation/graph/definition, no branch rewrite or completed receipt replacement, active identity retention, unknown/terminal stop, malformed/unknown/extra payload fields and codec, canonical encoding/digest failures, truncated/bounded snapshot rejection, unsupported atomic backend, and old/new reader compatibility. Unicode probes must distinguish scalar and explicit surrogate-pair IDs and preserve a lone surrogate. Guard import/use against provider/SDK initialization and credential reads; existing CP-8 stronger import isolation must remain green. Commands: `PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/test_skeleton*.py`, then relevant existing journal atomic-append tests and complete repository tests after focused pass. Stop with actual evidence for strong review and owner acceptance; no CP-10, commit, push, PR, audit or rollout.

### CP-9 Terra implementation result — ready for strong review (2026-09-27)

Created the explicit `unchain.skeleton.recovery_journal` adapter and retained CP-9 tests only; Skeleton `__init__`, CP-1–CP-8 sources, journal/store implementations, manifests and runtime wiring remain untouched. The adapter encodes closed CP-7 envelopes as canonical `json-utf8-surrogatepass-base64.v1`, validates every recovery-event header before graph filtering, checks the complete matching chain against the supplied prepared plan, and uses `append_with_artifacts(..., artifacts=(), precondition=...)` for replay-first transactional CAS. It preserves Unicode scalar, explicit-pair and lone-surrogate graph IDs, freezes generation/definition/invocation/call-stack, rejects rewrite/no-op/unknown/terminal advancement, and validates the projected bounded snapshot before commit. CP-9 remains descriptor persistence only: no resume, receipt payload access, provider/tool/sidecar/runtime integration or rollout is claimed.

Impact gate: `BoundExecutionJournal` reports MEDIUM risk (nine direct dependents; no listed process), consumed read-only. New adapter symbols remain unindexed/UNKNOWN; source search confirms no pre-existing consumers. Post-edit `detect-changes` reports no changes because these CP files are intentionally untracked in the isolated checkpoint, so that zero is not treated as graph evidence.

Write-time transition validation now checks immutable definition/invocation/call-stack fields before commit; the retained regression verifies rejection leaves one event. Review also corrected raw integer-decoder exceptions, silently normalized caller operation IDs, insufficiently strict persisted operation suffixes, and one-based event positions in diagnostics. All fixes stayed in the new adapter. Paths beneath `payload.encoded_envelope` refer to the decoded envelope's logical fields, not literal offsets within its base64 string.

Terra's 11 initial tests did not cover the complete frozen acceptance list. The strong parent took over the missing test slice rather than accepting the full-suite count as coverage. There are now **46 CP-9 cases**, including actual subprocess cold read; synchronized independent SQLite connections competing at revisions 0 and 1; retry after later progress; two successive waiting descriptors followed by End; interleaved graph identities; completed/branch/active/parent identity rewrites; unknown/terminal stops; foreign execution/attempt/generation/definition; malformed versions/closed fields/codec/digests/duplicate JSON names/huge integers; no fallback after a damaged tail; absence and detached reads; unsupported atomic journals; Unicode codepoint preservation; old/new reader refusal; real-store client/credential guards with negative controls; and actual 8 MiB encode/decode ceilings. The projected snapshot limit test deliberately lowers the shared journal cap while exercising the real SQLite transaction; it does not claim to have seeded 10,000 durable events. Overflow is rejected before write and a subsequent valid retry succeeds once the test cap is restored.

**BC-011 / SEQ-013 / AC-012 evidence, CP-9 subset only:** real producer -> existing SQLite event/object store -> fresh strict reader is verified for descriptor persistence. Four fresh-process mutation checks each produced the expected failing retained test when removing respectively the immutable-lineage guard, atomic precondition, payload schema check, or projected-capacity check; no source file was changed by these in-memory mutation probes. After restoration, the parent ran `PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/test_skeleton*.py tests/context_v2/test_journal_atomic_append.py`: **195 passed in 4.20s**. The subsequent full repository run passed: **3,903 passed, 16 skipped, 5 xfailed in 84.17s**. Both newly added files also pass direct whitespace checks; tracked/staged diffs remain empty, as all checkpoint files are intentionally untracked.

### CP-9 owner-requested acceptance — REQUEST CORRECTION (2026-09-27)

The parent independently reran CP-1–CP-9 plus the existing atomic-journal tests: **195 passed in 4.00s**, against the unchanged source/test hashes above. Six extra public-API process probes passed: commit then immediate `os._exit(0)` followed by a new process loading and exactly replaying waiting/unknown/terminal descriptors (three); synchronized independent processes submitting identical operations, the same operation with different bodies, or different operations at revision 0 (three). Each race persisted exactly one event, with duplicate or conflict behavior as appropriate. The earlier full-repository run was not repeated because no implementation changed. An independent read-only reviewer also inspected the core contract.

**P2 — Recovery event scope is validated after graph filtering, allowing contradictory identities to be hidden.** `recovery_journal.py:368–379` decodes graph keys and envelopes, then filters by graph. The self-contained operation digest, graph-scoped operation ID, and deterministic event identity are checked only later at lines 418–431 on matching records. Using only the real SQLite journal public append API, both reviewers reproduced: save a valid `graph` revision 1; append a canonical, correctly hashed descriptor whose decoded graph ID and graph key say `foreign-graph`, while its newly minted operation ID is scoped to `graph`. There are now two persisted events. Reading `graph` incorrectly returns revision 1; reading `absent` returns `None`; only reading `foreign-graph` rejects `identity_mismatch` at `("snapshot", "events", 1, "operation", "operation_id")`. No private mutation or direct database modification is required.

This violates the frozen BC-011 CP-9 rule that every recovery event's headers are validated before graph filtering so inconsistent identity cannot hide an event or permit fallback to older progress. Required correction: validate every event's self-contained operation content digest, operation graph scope, event ID and scope consistency against its own decoded envelope before filtering. Keep definition/plan-dependent validation restricted to the selected graph; valid foreign graphs with different definitions must continue to coexist. Retain negative cases for contradictory foreign headers when reading both the original and absent graphs, plus a positive coexistence case. No new storage design, active resume or CP-10 scope is required.

No production or test source was edited in this acceptance. CP-9 is not accepted until the finding is corrected and rechecked; no commit, push, PR or CP-10 work occurred.

### CP-9 acceptance correction — ready for re-acceptance (2026-09-27)

The reported hidden-identity defect is corrected in the isolated Unchain clone. Every recovery event now validates its self-contained attempt execution/attempt identity, operation content digest, operation-to-decoded-graph scope and deterministic event ID immediately after strict decoding and **before** graph filtering. Definition, call-stack transition and plan-dependent CP-7 validation remain limited to the selected graph. Missing foreign invocation identity produces a stable path-bearing `WorkflowRecoveryJournalError` rather than a native key error.

Three new behaviors are retained as four tests: a canonical foreign descriptor whose payload graph and operation graph contradict each other is rejected when querying either the original or an absent graph; a malformed foreign descriptor missing execution identity fails before filtering with a stable decoded-envelope path; and a valid foreign graph using a different Workflow definition coexists and can be read only with its own plan. The prior real SQLite reproducer now fails at `("snapshot", "events", 1, "operation", "operation_id")` before any fallback result.

No other CP-9 contract changed. Active resume, durable receipt payloads, Hook/child execution, sidecar wiring and deployed-artifact evidence remain later checkpoints / NOT_RUN. No main-tree edit, commit, push, PR, audit, rollout or CP-10 work occurred. Stop for owner re-acceptance.

The strong parent inspected the corrected pre-filter boundary and confirmed both source hashes still match the preceding correction entry. Twenty-one independent real SQLite public-API probes covered seven malformed foreign-event variants (operation graph scope, operation digest, event ID, execution mismatch, attempt mismatch, missing execution and missing attempt), each tested against the original graph read, absent graph read and a new save on the original graph.
