### CP15 implementation — locally complete, ready for independent acceptance (2026-09-29)

CP15 adds full Agent children to the existing subagent tools instead of creating a second child scheduler. A Subagent Pool now validates a CLOSED configuration, resolves referenced Agents by declared resource kind, admits embedded complete Agent documents, expands legacy inline/template entries through the strict converter, fixes the whole child definition closure before the first model call, and rejects missing, archived, wrong-kind, cyclic and over-depth descendants. A child shares the parent execution journal/content store while retaining its own graph/attempt identity and ephemeral memory namespace.

`delegate_to_subagent`, `handoff_to_subagent` and `spawn_worker` retain their existing public tools, communication state and legacy behavior. Full-graph children opt into graph-owned completion and a shared execution session. Successful handoff ends only the current Kernel invocation; the parent DAG continues through its mapped Code/End nodes, and only the parent End emits the root final message. Child errors retain their explicit error text. Legacy single-Agent delegate/worker manifests remain snapshot-only; terminal variants are added only for handoff or a declared full-graph child.

Child approval is durable across process loss. The child stores the authoritative interaction checkpoint; the parent stores a CLOSED child-wait request tied to the exact parent graph/node, child graph/attempt/node, definition digest, guard session and interaction ID. A cold parent reconstructs the same child identity, rejects wrong or replayed waits, resumes the child once, and continues the parent without another parent model request. The internal wait marker is exact JSON and is the only `awaiting_human_input` terminal handoff admitted by the Context transition. Kernel detects that exact marker and suspends without finalizing the child checkpoint. Cancelling the parent fences a waiting child so a later approval cannot execute the tool or publish a root reply.

Fresh implementation evidence:

- PuPu full-child scenarios — synchronous handoff, parent Code/End continuation, root-only final reply, child approval cold resume, replay/wrong-ID rejection, and parent cancellation while the child waits: **6 passed in 32.48s**.
- PuPu resource/Agent/root-completion group: **49 passed in 59.51s**.
- Unchain focused durable child, Kernel, transition, tool boundary and executor regression: **225 passed in 97.19s**; legacy subagent, approval authority, routes and mixed-runtime regression: **82 passed in 45.63s**.
- Complete Unchain suite: **4157 passed / 17 skipped / 5 expected failures in 264.33s**.
- PuPu sidecar full-suite probe reached **2526 passed / 17 skipped** but was not a clean suite result: 3 screenshot tests lacked Pillow, 5 Kimi tests lacked Anthropic, 7 multiprocessing tests were invalid under a stdin pytest launcher, and 2 subprocess tests lacked the isolated Unchain path. The 50 process/recovery/session tests were rerun through the normal module entry with `UNCHAIN_SOURCE_PATH` pinned to this clone and all passed in **16.77s**. Dependency failures are environment-only and outside CP15; they are not counted as CP15 acceptance evidence.
- Python compilation and tracked whitespace checks pass. A freshly restarted isolated sidecar using the paired PuPu/Unchain clones returned authenticated `/health` **200** and stopped cleanly.

### R9 / P1 — authoritative child End is not checked before creating the handoff

The new verification proves that a settlement matches the handoff just persisted, but that handoff was produced from the same received completion object. `kernel_runtime.py:690–701` selects the source attempt's last event without decoding its terminal state; `:707–740` writes `completion.outputs/status/error` as a new handoff without comparing them with the child's committed End artifact.

An independent expected-contract test reads the real child's final `skeleton.workflow_execution.saved` record, decodes its completed state, and reads its exact End output through the real artifact reader. The saved End and incoming authentic completion both contain `child done`. The fault changes only the host completion object immediately before `_persist_child_completion_input`; it leaves the child record and End bytes intact. The real handoff ingress writes a self-consistent new artifact containing `FORGED_CHILD_RESULT`, the new settlement verifier accepts it, and the fourth real provider-boundary request receives the forged result while the root completes.

This is an inconsistent trusted producer-to-persistence projection, not an external attacker exploit or a claim of same-process capability isolation. It extends the already-required R9 durable result-binding contract; no new isolation mechanism is requested.

The source cursor must identify the appropriate terminal receipt for that child invocation. Continue preserving call-bound settlement and existing handoff verification.

### R8 / P1 — crash after settlement append strands a known continuation

`durable_execution.py:585–588` changes the parent to running and clears its wait before resuming the handler. After the authentic handoff and original-call settlement have both committed, but before any parent continuation provider request, a process-loss exception leaves that state running. A fresh host, active bridge/factory, memory store and execution guard are constructed from the saved data. `recover()` cannot recognize the durable child settlement as a resumable continuation and seals `outcome_unknown` at `durable_execution.py:519–526`.

Fresh result: **1 negative failed in 9.25s** (`settlement-crash.xml`, `settlement-crash.log`). The child tool ran once, the child is completed, there is exactly one authentic settlement, and the cold client made zero requests. No durable record was fabricated or altered; the fault is a BaseException immediately after the real settlement append. The earlier six original-call/cold-resume controls remain green in the 22-test regression.

Required correction: retain a durable, recoverable child-settlement/parent-continuation intent across this transition and reconcile it before classifying an interrupted parent as outcome_unknown. Resume the next safe parent turn with its existing original-call, checkpoint, budget and provider ownership; do not replay completed child/model/tool operations. Preserve explicit uncertainty handling where a provider request actually may have been dispatched.

### CP15 R8/R9 correction — locally ready for independent re-acceptance (2026-09-30)

R9 now resolves the exact final `skeleton.workflow_execution.saved` record for the child attempt before a parent handoff can be created. It verifies the canonical state, terminal status, committed End receipt and immutable End output against the supplied completion. A host-only rewrite of the completion therefore cannot create a self-consistent but false handoff or settlement; the parent enters its existing safe unknown-outcome path without a fourth provider request.

R8 adds a closed, durable `resume: {kind: "child"}` marker only when a Kernel leaves a child wait to continue the parent. It is cleared before every wait, result or failure state is saved. Cold recovery accepts that marker only when it finds one valid settlement for the active parent attempt and no later tool-catalog, provider-wire or provider-result receipt. It then executes the one deferred parent continuation through the normal Kernel path. Any evidence that a request might already have started remains `outcome_unknown` and is never replayed.

### CP15 R8 correction — locally ready for independent re-acceptance (2026-09-30)

### CP16 implementation start — Hook Pool closed wire and decision contract (2026-09-30)

CP16 is now in progress in the paired `pupu-356` and `unchain-356` worktrees. The first boundary is a CLOSED Hook Pool producer wire from Agent Skeleton definition to the Skeleton-only Kernel tool runtime. `hooks_pool.config` is exactly `{hooks}`; every entry is exactly `{id,order,event,tool_name,workflow,force_approval}`. `event` is `before_tool_call` or `after_tool_call`; `tool_name` is a non-empty exact tool name or `null` for all tools; `workflow` is the fixed `{workflow_id,revision_id}` reference of one published non-LLM Workflow; `force_approval` is a boolean. Hook entries have unique IDs and run by `(order,id)`, so equal orders are deterministic. The resource resolver must prove the referenced revision has `kind=workflow`; Agent, latest/name fallback, unknown fields, unknown events and changed identity all fail closed. The first Unchain producer tests and Agent-preparation admission are green (**39 passed in 3.19s**), after two red import failures established that neither the config validator nor decision runtime existed.

**BC-004/006/011 extension, Hook admission.** Producer: Skeleton definition/resource preflight. Consumers: agent plan preparation, PuPu resource resolver and scoped Hook runtime. Representation is CLOSED; revision identity and definition digest are fixed with the enclosing Agent run snapshot. A Hook Pool is valid only when attached to its owning Agent Kernel; a standalone Tool Call, any nonattached pool, and child Hook Workflow tools do not inherit it. Final resource-kind enforcement is still pending its real resolver consumer; CP16 cannot claim this producer-only portion as full boundary evidence.

**BC-005/007/010/011 extension, Hook decision and projection.** A before child Workflow returns a CLOSED decision: `continue` with required boolean `require_approval` and optional replacement `arguments`, or `block` with a nonempty reason. There is intentionally no tool-ID field. A before replacement must be a JSON object; approval is accumulated by OR and block keeps the accumulated state but short-circuits parent tool execution. An after child returns either CLOSED `{decision:"continue"}` or CLOSED `{decision:"replace",result:<JSON object>}`. It receives immutable `raw_result` plus the current visible result; replacement becomes the only input to the next after Hook. The raw receipt remains audit-only. The current pure decision tests prove no later decision can restore a removed field. The durable child execution, final approval request, raw/after/visible receipts, compiler projection and cold recovery consumer are still pending.

**SEQ-009/010 planned order.** Fixed Hook manifest → before child receipts in sorted order → final-argument validation → one final parent approval when tool policy or any Hook requires it → tool intent/start/result receipt → after child receipts in sorted order → final visible-result receipt → compiler/model/binding/summary/artifact consumers. A wait/cancel/error in any child keeps the parent tool unexecuted or prevents subsequent stages; a completed stage is reused on recovery. A dispatched tool without a durable result remains outcome_unknown. The next implementation increment must intercept the Skeleton Kernel's tool call before `ToolExecutionHarness` prepares the original confirmation: a post-execution plugin cannot satisfy the required ordering. Generic Agent behavior stays untouched.

No live provider call, sidecar restart, commit, push, PR, rollout, ticket closure or Project mutation occurred. CP16 is not ready for acceptance; provider-backed Hook dispatch, nested approval, receipts/recovery and all final-visible consumer tests remain pending.

`SkeletonKernelHandler` now constructs `SkeletonHookRuntime` immediately after it has revalidated the immutable Agent plan and before it binds any Kernel profile. ### CP16 before-tool Hook authority — implementation increment in progress (2026-09-30)

**BC-005/006/011/012, SEQ-010, AC-013-B1–B4.** The first executable segment places before-tool Hook handling at the sole Context V2 tool-authority boundary, after the provider call has been durably identified and before native confirmation or the tool terminal handler can run. A Hook receives the closed event input `{event, call_id, tool_name, arguments}`; `arguments` are normalized with the same parser and JSON schema that the execution path uses. Each matching pinned Hook runs in stable admitted order. It may retain or replace only the arguments, monotonically demand parent approval, or block. It cannot replace the tool identity or choose the execution route.

The Context authority owns the exact result: it validates modified arguments again, combines native confirmation with `force_approval` using OR rather than the legacy resolver's potentially lowering policy, and presents the one final parent confirmation with final arguments. A block is a durable terminal tool result with `{error: {code, reason}, tool}` and no invocation of the actual tool handler. A modified approval response is also schema-validated before it can become effective arguments. The parent tool's execution subject continues to bind both original and effective argument digests.

PuPu now adapts an admitted pinned Workflow into a deterministic scoped child graph keyed by parent graph, Kernel invocation, call, Hook ID and pinned revision. It uses the normal durable Workflow runner and cloned non-LLM handlers; no Agent handler or enclosing Hook Pool is carried into that child. The retained host integration test runs a real Hook Start → Code → End graph and demonstrates that its output reaches the parent tool. The direct Context test covers forced parent approval, block with zero tool side effect, and schema-invalid Hook parameters with zero tool side effect.

This is deliberately not a completion claim for CP16. A Hook Workflow that waits, fails, is cancelled or becomes outcome-unknown currently fails closed before the parent tool executes; parent-visible Hook continuation, per-Hook receipts, cold recovery and after-tool result projection are the next CP16 segments. No live provider, sidecar restart, commit, push, PR, rollout, ticket closure or Project mutation occurred in this increment.

### CP16 Hook child Tool approval — local implementation increment (2026-09-30)

**BC-013 (VERSIONED, CLOSED).** The PuPu Hook host produces a durable child `WorkflowExecutionStore` wait using the official Unchain `SkeletonToolRuntime`; its child `InteractionRequest` is already a closed tool-approval request and remains the only authority to execute that child Tool Call. The host projects a closed `unchain.hook_child_wait.v1` envelope containing the pinned Hook/workflow/revision/definition digest, parent graph/node/tool call, child graph/attempt/node/wait identity, and the exact child request. `ContextRuntime` strictly validates that envelope, then produces a *parent-attempt* `tool_approval` interaction: the payload and response contract come from the child, while the subject binds the original parent tool intent cursor, route, schema, provider/model, and the entire child envelope. `KernelLoop` continuation pauses at the **parent tool call ID**, not the child call ID. Unknown fields, schema versions, mismatched identity, request digest or receipt are rejected; there is no fallback to a new model turn or a generic subagent completion. The consumer pair is `ContextRuntime`/`KernelLoop` for the parent receipt and PuPu/`SkeletonToolRuntime` for the child receipt. Actual source-tree pairing is locally tested; exact installed Unchain wheel + sidecar pairing remains `NOT_RUN` for CP17.

**SEQ-016 identity and persistence boundary (BC-011/013; AC-013-C1–C4).** The durable key is root execution/generation + graph/node attempt + child session + exact parent/child Tool call and wait IDs; the official guard chain is per-ownership-epoch authority, not a persisted key. The root journal owns provider, graph and Tool receipts; the child session owns the interaction checkpoint and projected live execution fence. Bootstrap binds the two through the real guard and host checkpoint. A same-guard renewal keeps its chain and advances no token backwards; a new root owner after a wait must prove the old owner is inactive, acquire a strictly newer token, and leave no pending Tool permit. Repeated approval and cold retry/resume/restart reuse committed effects; a changed sibling, root, attempt, wait, fence or published Hook revision fails before another effect. Reset creates a new run without transferring approval; rollback cannot consume an old approval or incompatible receipt. This corrects the earlier CP16 use of `SEQ-012`, which remains the FYI/BTW interjection sequence.

**SEQ-016 / AC-013-C1–C4.** The ordered states are: original provider turn and parent tool intent → child Hook Tool approval wait → parent-attempt interaction request/receipt → exact child approval receipt and one child Tool execution → separate parent-tool approval request/receipt (if Hook demands it) → one parent Tool execution → End. Parent and child wait IDs remain distinct. Repeated/stale wait IDs fail before any new effect; cold host and memory-store reconstruction resumes from the original pending provider tool call without another provider request. Child denial must not execute either tool or complete the root. A changed Hook revision, child request, parent tool identity, provider/model/tool schema, or receipt fails closed. Reset does not erase an external Tool effect; rollback cannot reuse an old approval for a changed intent. The direct-child path and cold reconstruction have permanent positive/negative tests; nested Workflow Call child waits, per-Hook phase receipts, after-Hook projection, process-restart fault injection, real provider and deployed artifact-pair evidence remain pending CP16/CP17 acceptance, so this paragraph is an implementation record, not acceptance.

### CP16 Hook phase and after-Tool recovery contract — implementation in progress (2026-10-02)

**BC-014 / SEQ-017 / AC-013-D1–D6.** This increment crosses the Kernel provider turn → Tool result → parent journal → Hook child Workflow → Context compiler boundary. The producer is Unchain `DurableToolExecutor` and the admitted `SkeletonAfterToolHookDispatcher`; the strict consumers are `DurableToolBoundary`, `HookRawSealStore`, `HookPhaseJournal`, `ContextRuntime`, and the cold `SkeletonKernelHandler`. The producer writes a CLOSED `tool.hook_raw.sealed` event containing exact parent graph/node invocation, Kernel attempt, Tool call/iteration, durable execution subject, pinned Hook manifest digest and one binary artifact descriptor. Its canonical audit artifact contains the raw result, original completion state and effective arguments; its preview is empty. It is an indexed Tool seal, not a `tool_result` or compiler input. Each completed Hook writes one CLOSED `skeleton.hook_phase.receipt` with pinned workflow ID/revision/definition digest, input SHA-256 and closed decision. Unknown fields, wrong schema/version, changed event operation, artifact bytes, Tool subject, Hook revision or phase identity fail before projection. The final visible `tool_result` is persisted only after every after Hook finishes, and downstream model/Context consumers use that receipt rather than the raw seal. No after Hook uses a model; Hooks remain child Workflows.

**SEQ-017.** The durable key is root execution/generation + parent graph/node attempt + Tool call/iteration + Hook ID/revision. Sequence: verified provider turn and Tool intent → before-Hook child and per-phase receipt → final-argument approval → one Tool start/effect → raw audit seal → ordered after-Hook child/receipt(s) → final visible Tool receipt → later model turn/End. A crash before raw seal remains `outcome_unknown` and never re-executes the Tool. A crash after raw seal resumes the exact verified provider Tool batch without another provider send or Tool effect; a crash after child End but before its phase receipt reuses the durable child End. Child Tool approval suspends the parent even after the parent Tool effect, then resumes only the remaining Hook and visible projection. Repeated/stale approval, changed Hook/Tool identity or corrupted seal fails closed. A completed earlier Tool turn is excluded when selecting a later unfinished turn. After the first replace, later Hooks receive only the current visible result and an empty `raw_result`; the audit artifact remains available for explicit audit, not the model or ordinary next Hook.

**AC-013-D1–D6 evidence so far:** real PuPu host tests cover live replacement and raw-sentinel isolation from later model requests/public `tool_result`; strict consumer rejection of extra field, wrong version, wrong call ID and wrong operation; two-Hook redaction order, deterministic second-Hook failure and crash after H1 receipt without H1 re-execution; child approval/denial/cancellation after the parent Tool with cold reconstruction; crash before/after raw seal, after the after-phase child End, and before the final visible result; crash after a before-phase receipt; second Tool turn recovery; deterministic invalid after decision preserving the raw Tool receipt without model continuation; unknown child result stopping model continuation; terminal handoff only after its after Hook; and nested Workflow Call Hook recovery. The final Unchain suite passed 4205 tests (17 skipped, 5 xfailed) after its product-source changes. A final PuPu host/approval matrix is running against the latest tests. Remaining CP16 completion work includes every Context/summary/binding/artifact consumer, final PuPu matrix, and independent acceptance. Exact deployed wheel + sidecar pair remains CP17 and active rollout remains `INCOMPLETE`. No live sidecar was used; changed `.py` modules require restart before manual app validation. No commit, push, PR, rollout, ticket closure or Project mutation occurred.

### CP17 R3 草稿 IO 修复 — 独立验收 FAIL（2026-10-06）

验收对象是上节本地源码候选，HEAD 和未提交源码内容一并冻结，未接管 Claude UI 或进入实机。**两个新 P2 使 R3 切片 FAIL；另有一个既有 P2 strict 准入缺口被这轮诊断暴露。** 原 R3 三个负例已修复，不以既有测试通过代替新增边界负例。

**R3-A / P2：冷进程诊断不确定。** `draft_io._rows` 沿用 strict `_closed_object` 遍历 required set 的首错。相同 schema 合法文档的 `inputs:[{}]`、`outputs:[{}]` 在 12 个 Python hash seed 下得到六种 CLOSED 响应；seed 0 定位 missing/source，seed 1 定位 value/name。嵌套残缺 ref 同样在 node_id/path 间变动。该 strict helper 原有，但新权威草稿 producer 继承它后违反 SEQ-019 的 repeat/cold-read 一致性；修复需稳定外层及嵌套对象的字段选择/收集，保留 strict 错误顺序要求。

**R3-B / P2：将类型上不存在的 IO surface 当成尚未配置。** 已完整配置 If/Switch 没有 outputs section，导出恒为空对象；当前引用检查跳过，因此 End 必需引用 `ref(if,["payload"])` 得到空诊断，真 `run_workflow` 必然 missing_value。Start 没有 binding inputs，输出 source=input/path=["payload"] 被同类 guard 跳过，首步实际执行失败。属于 AC-016-D3 静态导出/映射检查缺口，不是运行期动态叶值。对应合法 path=[] 两个对照都实际返回 `{answer:{}}`；修复不得误拒绝它们或有效条件 first_present。

**R3-C / P2：已知必然失败的静态引用仍可 preflight/publish（既有缺口，非本轮回归）。** 四类 required direct ref（未知节点、自身、下游、未导出名称）经真实 store save/get/diagnose 报 missing_value，却 preflight `ok:true,diagnostics:[]`，且实际持久化 rev-1。相同图实际运行在 Code handler 前失败。旧 `_validate_execution` 仅结构/IO grammar 准入，未消费这些静态约束；保持旧路径不等于满足 D-07 的完整 Run/Publish 校验。该项是完整 CP17 准入遗留项，下一轮应明确收敛；修复须保留 Kernel 错误先后、条件 first_present 与 missing 策略，不能将所有 draft diagnostic 提前一律拒绝。

### CP17 / BC-021 recursive Workflow admission R1/R2 repair — ready for independent re-acceptance (2026-10-09)

落实 BC-021 / SEQ-024 / AC-021-A–F 的两项修复。**R1 identity:** Store preflight now rejects surrounding whitespace in authored `workflow_id` and `revision_id` at the exact field path, without rewriting the saved document or changing generic `_text` behavior. This keeps authoring, dependency metadata, freeze lookup, and the official WorkflowCall identity check on one exact spelling. Retained negative cases cover both Workflow Call and Hook references and both identity fields; exact-reference controls still resolve the published immutable version.

**R2 depth:** Store admission and frozen snapshot resolution now count the executing root graph as frame one and propagate the frame offset and graph-ID ancestry through nested Workflow Calls, child Agents, and their Hook references. The hard limit remains the official runtime maximum of 32 total frames. Therefore a root Agent may reference at most 31 nested Workflow definitions; an embedded child Agent Hook may reference at most 30. Cached Workflow subtrees are checked at each deeper placement, so a shallow first visit cannot mask an over-limit later path. Workflow publication likewise includes the published root Workflow frame. The runtime limit was not changed.

Red-before-green evidence: the four original whitespace cases, root Agent 32-reference boundary, child-Agent Hook 31-reference boundary, and shared-subtree deeper placement reproduced **7 failures** with the two controls passing; after the repair the new cases pass. The retained publisher-depth regression asserts the root-inclusive 31/32 boundary; extra permanent negatives cover whitespace rejection during Workflow publish and the freezer's rejection of a deeply placed shared subtree when given a strict selection envelope directly. Final resource/snapshot regression passed **66 tests**; chat selection, Agent runtime, and Hook approval/recovery regression passed **178 tests in 526.15s**. Those runs used the ticket clones' source pair, local Python environment, no provider smoke, and no installed sidecar.

Only the isolated PuPu ticket clone was changed: `skeleton_resource_store.py`, `skeleton_workflow_snapshot.py`, the dependency snapshot tests, the existing resource-store depth test, and this plan. The matching Unchain source tree and main checkouts were not changed. Existing working-tree changes from earlier CP17 slices remain in place. GitNexus impact was run before editing the affected collectors/freezer and source-correlated where the stale graph could not resolve new symbols; no graph all-clear is claimed. The final repair artifact records the original R1/R2 failures, commands, outcomes, hashes, and limitations at `/Users/red/.codex/artifacts/pupu-356/cp17-recursive-dependency-repair-20261009/repair-report.md`.

This is a local source-pair repair awaiting independent acceptance, not full CP17 acceptance. Keep #356 open. Exact installed wheel/sidecar pairing, real providers, desktop/UI, sidecar restart, production chat, and rollout remain paused or `NOT_RUN`; Python changes require restarting the matching sidecar before future manual qualification. No commit, push, PR, release/Project mutation, rollout, or ticket closure occurred. Stop here for independent re-acceptance.
